The vacuum stops being empty and starts acting like a polarizable material. This is the framework Born–Infeld lives in; here we build the machinery, and Born-Infeld action picks the one \\mathcal L that string theory demands."}],["$","dl",null,{"className":"article-byline","children":[["$","div",null,{"children":[["$","dt",null,{"children":"Source"}],["$","dd",null,{"children":"AI-written"}]]}],["$","div",null,{"children":[["$","dt",null,{"children":"Status"}],["$","dd",null,{"children":"To learn"}]]}],["$","div",null,{"children":[["$","dt",null,{"children":"Published"}],["$","dd",null,{"children":"Jun 30, 2026"}]]}],["$","div",null,{"children":[["$","dt",null,{"children":"Reading time"}],["$","dd",null,{"children":[10," min · ","2,065"," words"]}]]}]]}]]}],["$","details",null,{"className":"mobile-toc","children":[["$","summary",null,{"children":"On this page"}],["$","ol",null,{"children":[["$","li","the-split-vec-evec-b-vs-vec-dvec-h",{"className":"toc-depth-2","children":["$","a",null,{"href":"#the-split-vec-evec-b-vs-vec-dvec-h","children":"The split: (\\vec E,\\vec B) vs (\\vec D,\\vec H)"}]}],["$","li","covariant-form-the-tensor-gmunu",{"className":"toc-depth-2","children":["$","a",null,{"href":"#covariant-form-the-tensor-gmunu","children":"Covariant form: the tensor G^{\\mu\\nu}"}]}],["$","li","the-master-formula-gmunu---partialmathcal-lpartial-f_munu",{"className":"toc-depth-2","children":["$","a",null,{"href":"#the-master-formula-gmunu---partialmathcal-lpartial-f_munu","children":"The master formula: G^{\\mu\\nu} = -\\partial\\mathcal L/\\partial F_{\\mu\\nu}"}]}],["$","li","reading-off-vec-d-and-vec-h",{"className":"toc-depth-2","children":["$","a",null,{"href":"#reading-off-vec-d-and-vec-h","children":"Reading off \\vec D and \\vec H"}]}],["$","li","what-can-mathcal-l-depend-on-the-two-invariants",{"className":"toc-depth-2","children":["$","a",null,{"href":"#what-can-mathcal-l-depend-on-the-two-invariants","children":"What can \\mathcal L depend on? Nonlinear electrodynamics keeps every Maxwell equation but replaces the law by a free function <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi mathvariant="script">L</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathcal L</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathcal">L</span></span></span></span> — and applies it to the vacuum itself. Everything below unpacks that one move.</p>
<h2 id="the-split-vec-evec-b-vs-vec-dvec-h">The split: <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mover accent="true"><mi>E</mi><mo>⃗</mo></mover><mo separator="true">,</mo><mover accent="true"><mi>B</mi><mo>⃗</mo></mover><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(\vec E,\vec B)</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1.2163em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord accent"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.9663em;"><span style="top:-3em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0576em;">E</span></span><span style="top:-3.2523em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="accent-body" style="left:-0.1522em;"><span class="overlay" style="height:0.714em;width:0.471em;"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="0.471em" height="0.714em" style="width:0.471em" viewBox="0 0 471 714" preserveAspectRatio="xMinYMin"><path d="M377 20c0-5.333 1.833-10 5.5-14S391 0 397 0c4.667 0 8.667 1.667 12 5

<p>In a medium Maxwell's equations split into two groups (we set <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>c</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">c=1</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">c</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">1</span></span></span></span> throughout). The <strong>source-free</strong> pair:</p>
<p><span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">∇</mi><mo>×</mo><mover accent="true"><mi>E</mi><mo>⃗</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mo>−</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><mover accent="true"><mi>B</mi><mo>⃗</mo></mover></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo separator="true">,</mo><mspace width="2em"></mspace><mi mathvariant="normal">∇</mi><mo>⋅</mo><mover accent="true"><mi>B</mi><mo>⃗</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo separator="true">,</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\nabla\times\vec E = -\frac{\partial\vec B}{\partial t}, \qquad \nabla\cdot\vec B = 0,</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7667em;vertical-align:-0.0833em;"></span><span class="mord">∇</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span><span class="mbin">×</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.9663em;"></span><span class="mord accent"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.9663em;"><span style="top:-3em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0576em;">E</span></span><span style="top:-3.2523em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="accent-body" style="left:-0.1522em;"><span class="overlay" style="height:0.714em;width:0.471em;"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="0.471em" height="0.714em" style="width:0.471em" viewBox="0 0 471 714" preserveAspectRatio="xMinYMin"><path d="M377 20c0-5.333 1.833-10 5.5-14S391 0 397 0c4.667 0 8.667 1.667 12 5

Nonlinear electrodynamics is the case where this rule is nonlinear, and applied to the vacuum. The conceptual leap is that the <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mover accent="true"><mi>E</mi><mo>⃗</mo></mover><mo separator="true">,</mo><mover accent="true"><mi>B</mi><mo>⃗</mo></mover><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(\vec E,\vec B)</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1.2163em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord accent"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.9663em;"><span style="top:-3em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0576em;">E</span></span><span style="top:-3.2523em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="accent-body" style="left:-0.1522em;"><span class="overlay" style="height:0.714em;width:0.471em;"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="0.471em" height="0.714em" style="width:0.471em" viewBox="0 0 471 714" preserveAspectRatio="xMinYMin"><path d="M377 20c0-5.333 1.833-10 5.5-14S391 0 397 0c4.667 0 8.667 1.667 12 5

The source-free pair is the automatic Bianchi identity. The sourced pair is written with a <em>new</em> antisymmetric tensor <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>G</mi><mrow><mi>μ</mi><mi>ν</mi></mrow></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G^{\mu\nu}</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">G</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.6644em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0637em;">μν</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span>:</p>
<p><span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><msup><mi>G</mi><mrow><mi>μ</mi><mi>ν</mi></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mi <p>In Maxwell theory <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>G</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>F</mi></mrow><annotation class="mord mathnormal">G</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">F</span></span></span></span> and this is just <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><mi>ν</mi></msub><msup><mi>F</mi><mrow><mi>μ</mi><mi>ν</mi></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><msup><mi>j</mi><mi>μ</mi></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\partial_\nu F^{\mu\nu}=j^\mu</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.1514em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:-0.0556em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0637em;">ν</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">F</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.6644em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">μ</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span>. In a nonlinear theory <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>G</mi><mo mathvariant="normal">≠</mo><mi>F</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G\ne F</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8889em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">G</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel"><span class="mrel"><span class="mord vbox"><span class="thinbox"><span class="rlap"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8889em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="inner"><span class="mord"><span class="mrel"></span></span></span><span class="fix"></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mspace nobreak"></span><span class="mrel">=</span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">F</span></span></span></span>, and the entire content of the nonlinearity sits in the function <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>G</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>F</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G(F)</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">G</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">F</span><span class="mclose">)</span></span></span></span>. The rest of the note produces that function from a Lagrangian.</p>
<h2 id="the-master-formula-gmunu---partialmathcal-lpartial-f_munu">The master formula: <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>G</mi><mrow><mi>μ</mi><mi>ν</mi></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mo>−</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><mi mathvariant="script">L</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">/</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><msub><mi>F</mi><mrow><mi>μ</mi><mi>ν</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><annotation class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">G</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.6644em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span style="margin-right:0.0556em;">∂</span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">F</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.1514em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:-0.1389em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0637em;">μν</span></span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span <p>Where does <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>G</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">G</span></span></span></span> come from? From an action. Take a Lagrangian density <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi mathvariant="script">L</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mi>F</mi><mrow><mi>μ</mi><mi>ν</mi></mrow></msub><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathcal L(F_{\mu\nu})</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1.0361em;vertical-align:-0.2861em;"></span><span class="mord mathcal">L</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">F</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.1514em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:-0.1389em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">F</span></span></span></span>, and <strong>local</strong> because it uses <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>F</mi></mrow><annotation xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><mi>ρ</mi></msub><msub><mi>F</mi><mrow><mi>μ</mi><mi>ν</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><annotation <p><span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8413em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="margin-right:0.0278em;">D</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mord mathnormal">x</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">A</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.1514em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:0em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">μ</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.2861em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0572em;">j</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.6644em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">μ</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mord">.</span></span></span></span></p>
<p>Varying <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi mathvariant="script">L</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathcal L</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathcal">L</span></span></span></span> means differentiating with respect to the <em>antisymmetric</em> object <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>F</mi><mrow><mi>μ</mi><mi>ν</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">F_{\mu\nu}</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.9694em;vertical-align:-0.2861em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">F</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.1514em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:-0.1389em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0637em;">μν</span></span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.2861em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span>, which needs one convention. For any function <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>M</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">M</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.109em;">M</span></span></span></span> of the field strengths,</p>
<p><span class="katex-error" title="ParseError: KaTeX parse error: \tag works only in display equations" style="color:#cc0000"> \delta M = \frac{1}{2}\frac{\partial M}{\partial F_{\mu\nu}}\,\delta F_{\mu\nu}, \qquad \frac{\partial M}{\partial F_{\mu\nu}} = -\frac{\partial M}{\partial F_{\nu\mu}}. \tag{1}</span></p>
<p>The <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mstyle scriptlevel="0" displaystyle="false"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mstyle></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\tfrac12</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1.1901em;vertical-align:-0.345em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mopen nulldelimiter"></span><span class="mfrac"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8451em;"><span style="top:-2.655em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mtight">2</span></span></span></span><span style="top:-3.23em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="frac-line" style="border-bottom-width:0.04em;"></span></span><span style="top:-3.394em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mtight">1</span></span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.345em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mclose nulldelimiter"></span></span></span></span></span> undoes the double-counting from summing over both orderings <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>μ</mi><mi>ν</mi><mo and <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>ν</mi><mi>μ</mi><mo style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0637em;">ν</span><span class="mord mathnormal">μ</span><span class="mclose">)</span></span></span></span>; the antisymmetry of <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><mi>M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">/</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><msub><mi>F</mi><mrow><mi>μ</mi><mi>ν</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\partial M/\partial F_{\mu\nu}</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1.0361em;vertical-align:-0.2861em;"></span><span class="mord" style="margin-right:0.0556em;">∂</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.109em;">M</span><span class="mord">/</span><span class="mord" style="margin-right:0.0556em;">∂</span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">F</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.1514em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:-0.1389em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0637em;">μν</span></span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" Now plug in <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>δ</mi><msub><mi>F</mi><mrow><mi>μ</mi><mi>ν</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi A_\mu</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.9805em;vertical-align:-0.2861em;"></span><span <p><span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math <p><span class="katex-error" title="ParseError: KaTeX parse error: \tag works only in display equations" style="color:#cc0000"> \boxed{\;G^{\mu\nu} = -\frac{\partial\mathcal L}{\partial F_{\mu\nu}}\;}. \tag{2}</span></p>
<p>This is the engine of the framework: name any <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi mathvariant="script">L</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>F</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathcal L(F)</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord mathcal">L</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">F</span><span class="mclose">)</span></span></span></span> and (2) hands you <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>G</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">G</span></span></span></span>, hence the constitutive law. Choosing <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi mathvariant="script">L</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathcal L</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathcal">L</span></span></span></span> is choosing a theory.</p>

<p>Apply (2) componentwise. <p><span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><menclose notation="box"><mstyle scriptlevel="0" displaystyle="false"><mstyle scriptlevel="0" displaystyle="false"><mstyle scriptlevel="0" displaystyle="true"><mrow><mtext>  </mtext><mover accent="true"><mi>H</mi><mo>⃗</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mo>−</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><mi mathvariant="script">L</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><mover accent="true"><mi>B</mi><mo>⃗</mo></mover></mrow></mfrac><mtext>  </mtext></mrow></mstyle></mstyle></mstyle></menclose><mi mathvariant="normal">.</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\boxed{\;\vec H = -\frac{\partial\mathcal L}{\partial\vec B}\;}.</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:2.9078em;vertical-align:-1.1963em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:1.7114em;"><span style="top:-4.9078em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:4.9078em;"></span><span class="boxpad"><span class="mord"><span class="mord"><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mord accent"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.9663em;"><span style="top:-3em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0813em;">H</span></span><span style="top:-3.2523em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="accent-body" style="left:-0.1799em;"><span class="overlay" style="height:0.714em;width:0.471em;"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="0.471em" height="0.714em" style="width:0.471em" viewBox="0 0 471 714" preserveAspectRatio="xMinYMin"><path d="M377 20c0-5.333 1.833-10 5.5-14S391 0 397 0c4.667 0 8.667 1.667 12 5

Maxwell is the one case where the vacuum is not polarizable; any nonlinear <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi mathvariant="script">L</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathcal L</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathcal">L</span></span></span></span> makes <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mover accent="true"><mi>D</mi><mo>⃗</mo></mover><mo mathvariant="normal">≠</mo><mover accent="true"><mi>E</mi><mo>⃗</mo></mover></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\vec D\ne\vec E</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1.1608em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord accent"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.9663em;"><span style="top:-3em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0278em;">D</span></span><span style="top:-3.2523em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="accent-body" style="left:-0.1799em;"><span class="overlay" style="height:0.714em;width:0.471em;"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="0.471em" height="0.714em" style="width:0.471em" viewBox="0 0 471 714" preserveAspectRatio="xMinYMin"><path d="M377 20c0-5.333 1.833-10 5.5-14S391 0 397 0c4.667 0 8.667 1.667 12 5

<h2 id="what-can-mathcal-l-depend-on-the-two-invariants">What can <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi mathvariant="script">L</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathcal L</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathcal">L</span></span></span></span> depend on? The two invariants</h2>
<p>Gauge invariance is free. Lorentz invariance is the real constraint: <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi mathvariant="script">L</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathcal L</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathcal">L</span></span></span></span> must be a Lorentz scalar built from <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>F</mi><mrow><mi>μ</mi><mi>ν</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">F_{\mu\nu}</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.9694em;vertical-align:-0.2861em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">F</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.1514em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:-0.1389em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0637em;">μν</span></span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.2861em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> with no free indices. In four dimensions there are <strong>exactly two</strong> independent such scalars:</p>
<p><span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>s</mi><mo>≡</mo><mo>−</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac><msup><mi>F</mi><mrow><mi>μ</mi><mi>ν</mi></mrow></msup><msub><mi>F</mi><mrow><mi>μ</mi><mi>ν</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msup><mi>E</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>−</mo><msup><mi>B</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo separator="true">,</mo><mspace width="2em"></mspace><mi>p</mi><mo>≡</mo><mo>−</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac><msup><mover accent="true"><mi>F</mi><mo>~</mo></mover><mrow><mi>μ</mi><mi>ν</mi></mrow></msup><msub><mi>F</mi><mrow><mi>μ</mi><mi>ν</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mover <p>where <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mover <p><span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><menclose notation="box"><mstyle scriptlevel="0" displaystyle="false"><mstyle aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1.68em;vertical-align:-0.59em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:1.09em;"><span style="top:-3.68em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3.68em;"></span><span class="boxpad"><span class="mord"><span class="mord"><span class="mspace" <p>Maxwell is simply <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi mathvariant="script">L</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>s</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathcal L = s</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathcal">L</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">s</span></span></span></span>. Everything else — Born–Infeld included — is a different choice of this function. Note <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>s</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">s</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">s</span></span></span></span> is a true scalar but <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>p</mi><mo>=</mo><mover accent="true"><mi>E</mi><mo>⃗</mo></mover><mo>⋅</mo><mover accent="true"><mi>B</mi><mo>⃗</mo></mover></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">p=\vec E\cdot\vec B</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.625em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.9663em;"></span><span class="mord accent"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.9663em;"><span style="top:-3em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0576em;">E</span></span><span style="top:-3.2523em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="accent-body" style="left:-0.1522em;"><span class="overlay" style="height:0.714em;width:0.471em;"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="0.471em" height="0.714em" style="width:0.471em" viewBox="0 0 471 714" preserveAspectRatio="xMinYMin"><path d="M377 20c0-5.333 1.833-10 5.5-14S391 0 397 0c4.667 0 8.667 1.667 12 5

c-16-25.333-24-45-24-59z"></path></svg></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span><span class="mbin">⋅</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.9663em;"></span><span class="mord accent"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.9663em;"><span style="top:-3em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0502em;">B</span></span><span style="top:-3.2523em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="accent-body" style="left:-0.1522em;"><span class="overlay" style="height:0.714em;width:0.471em;"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="0.471em" height="0.714em" style="width:0.471em" viewBox="0 0 471 714" preserveAspectRatio="xMinYMin"><path d="M377 20c0-5.333 1.833-10 5.5-14S391 0 397 0c4.667 0 8.667 1.667 12 5

c-16-25.333-24-45-24-59z"></path></svg></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> is a <em>pseudo</em>scalar (it flips sign under parity), so a parity-invariant theory can use <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">p</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.625em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span></span></span></span> only through <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>p</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">p^2</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1.0085em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8141em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight">2</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span>.</p>
<p>Why the list stops at two. Given a generic (non-null) electromagnetic field, you can always find a frame where <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mover accent="true"><mi>E</mi><mo>⃗</mo></mover></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\vec E</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.9663em;"></span><span class="mord accent"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.9663em;"><span style="top:-3em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0576em;">E</span></span><span style="top:-3.2523em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="accent-body" style="left:-0.1522em;"><span class="overlay" style="height:0.714em;width:0.471em;"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="0.471em" height="0.714em" style="width:0.471em" viewBox="0 0 471 714" preserveAspectRatio="xMinYMin"><path d="M377 20c0-5.333 1.833-10 5.5-14S391 0 397 0c4.667 0 8.667 1.667 12 5

10.667 11 18 0 6-1 10-3 12s-6.667 5-14 9c-28.667 14.667-53.667 35.667-75 63
-1.333 1.333-3.167 3.5-5.5 6.5s-4 4.833-5 5.5c-1 .667-2.5 1.333-4.5 2s-4.333 1
-7 1c-4.667 0-9.167-1.833-13.5-5.5S337 184 337 178c0-12.667 15.667-32.333 47-59
H213l-171-1c-8.667-6-13-12.333-13-19 0-4.667 4.333-11.333 13-20h359
c-16-25.333-24-45-24-59z"></path></svg></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> and <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mover accent="true"><mi>B</mi><mo>⃗</mo></mover></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\vec B</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.9663em;"></span><span class="mord accent"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.9663em;"><span style="top:-3em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0502em;">B</span></span><span style="top:-3.2523em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="accent-body" style="left:-0.1522em;"><span class="overlay" style="height:0.714em;width:0.471em;"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="0.471em" height="0.714em" style="width:0.471em" viewBox="0 0 471 714" preserveAspectRatio="xMinYMin"><path d="M377 20c0-5.333 1.833-10 5.5-14S391 0 397 0c4.667 0 8.667 1.667 12 5

-7 1c-4.667 0-9.167-1.833-13.5-5.5S337 184 337 178c0-12.667 15.667-32.333 47-59
H213l-171-1c-8.667-6-13-12.333-13-19 0-4.667 4.333-11.333 13-20h359
c-16-25.333-24-45-24-59z"></path></svg></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> are <em>parallel</em> — boosting mixes them until the perpendicular parts cancel. In that frame the field is described by just two magnitudes, <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>E</mi><mo>∥</mo></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">E_\parallel</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1.0385em;vertical-align:-0.3552em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0576em;">E</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3448em;"><span mtight">∥</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3552em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> and <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>B</mi><mo>∥</mo></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">B_\parallel</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1.0385em;vertical-align:-0.3552em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0502em;">B</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3448em;"><span style="top:-2.5198em;margin-left:-0.0502em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mrel mtight">∥</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3552em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span>, and</p>
<p><span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msubsup><mi>E</mi><mo>∥</mo><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>−</mo><msubsup><mi>B</mi><mo>∥</mo><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>s</mi><mo separator="true">,</mo><mspace width="2em"></mspace><msub><mi>E</mi><mo>∥</mo></msub><msub><mi>B</mi><mo>∥</mo></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>p</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">.</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">E_\parallel^2 - B_\parallel^2 = 2s, \qquad E_\parallel B_\parallel = p.</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1.3111em;vertical-align:-0.497em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0576em;">E</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8141em;"><span style="top:-2.378em;margin-left:-0.0576em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mrel mtight">∥</span></span></span><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.497em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span><span class="mbin">−</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1.3111em;vertical-align:-0.497em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0502em;">B</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8141em;"><span mtight">∥</span></span></span><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord style="margin-right:2em;"></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0576em;">E</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3448em;"><span style="top:-2.5198em;margin-left:-0.0576em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mrel mtight">∥</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3552em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" mtight">∥</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3552em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span style="height:0.625em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span><span class="mord">.</span></span></span></span></p>
<p>Any Lorentz scalar built algebraically from <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>F</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">F</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">F</span></span></span></span> is a frame-independent number, so it can depend only on these two frame-independent magnitudes — on <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>s</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">s</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">s</span></span></span></span> and <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">p</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.625em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span></span></span></span>. There is nothing else. (Degenerate cases <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>s</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">s=0</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">s</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">0</span></span></span></span> or <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>p</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">p=0</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.625em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">0</span></span></span></span> follow by continuity.) That is why <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>s</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">s</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">s</span></span></span></span> and <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">p</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.625em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span></span></span></span> are the only invariants in four dimensions.</p>
<p>One caveat on locality. All of this assumes <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi mathvariant="script">L</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathcal L</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathcal">L</span></span></span></span> is algebraic in <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>F</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">F</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">F</span></span></span></span>. Derivative terms like <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><mi>ρ</mi></msub><msub><mi>F</mi><mrow><mi>μ</mi><mi>ν</mi></mrow></msub><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><mi>ρ</mi></msup><msup><mi>F</mi><mrow><mi>μ</mi><mi>ν</mi></mrow></msup><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(\partial_\rho F_{\mu\nu})(\partial^\rho F^{\mu\nu})</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1.0361em;vertical-align:-0.2861em;"></span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord"><span class="mord" style="margin-right:0.0556em;">∂</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.1514em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:-0.0556em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">ρ</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.2861em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">F</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.1514em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:-0.1389em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0637em;">μν</span></span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.2861em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mclose">)</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord"><span class="mord" style="margin-right:0.0556em;">∂</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.6644em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">ρ</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">F</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.6644em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0637em;">μν</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mclose">)</span></span></span></span> are also legal, gauge-invariant, and Lorentz-invariant, but they live <em>outside</em> this framework. Born–Infeld and its neighbors are the local, algebraic <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi mathvariant="script">L</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>s</mi><mo separator="true">,</mo><mi>p</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathcal L(s,p)</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord mathcal">L</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal">s</span><span class="mpunct">,</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span><span class="mclose">)</span></span></span></span>; the <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><mi>F</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\partial F</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em;"></span><span class="mord" style="margin-right:0.0556em;">∂</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">F</span></span></span></span> corrections are a separate story, and the first thing this framework misses.</p>
<h2 id="why-this-is-the-right-home-for-the-brane-gauge-field">Why this is the right home for the brane gauge field</h2>
<p>Two demands pick out the brane theory from the infinite space of <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi mathvariant="script">L</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>s</mi><mo separator="true">,</mo><mi>p</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathcal L(s,p)</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord mathcal">L</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal">s</span><span class="mpunct">,</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span><span class="mclose">)</span></span></span></span>:</p>
<ol>
<li><strong>Reduce to Maxwell at weak field</strong> — because quantizing open strings already produced a genuine Maxwell photon on the brane (<a href="/rome-wiki/articles/string-theory/part-iv-branes-and-duality/19-string-charge-and-gauge-fields/endpoint-charge/" class="wiki-link">module 19</a>). So <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi mathvariant="script">L</mi><mo>→</mo><mi>s</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathcal L \to s</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathcal">L</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">→</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">s</span></span></span></span> as <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>F</mi><mo>→</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">F\to 0</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">F</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">→</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">0</span></span></span></span>.</li>
<li><strong>A maximal electric field</strong> <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>E</mi><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">/</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi><msup><mi>α</mi><mo mathvariant="normal" lspace="0em" rspace="0em">′</mo></msup><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo>≡</mo><mi>b</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">E_{\rm crit}=1/(2\pi\alpha')\equiv b</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8333em;vertical-align:-0.15em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0576em;">E</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3175em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:-0.0576em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mathrm mtight">crit</span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1.0019em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord">1/</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord">2</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0359em;">π</span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0037em;">α</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.7519em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mtight">′</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mclose">)</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">≡</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">b</span></span></span></span> — because a charged string endpoint cannot be pulled harder than the string tension (<a href="/rome-wiki/articles/string-theory/part-iv-branes-and-duality/21-born-infeld-and-brane-dynamics/electric-and-magnetic-fields-on-d-branes/" class="wiki-link">Electric and magnetic fields on D-branes</a>). Maxwell has no such scale, so the theory <em>must</em> be nonlinear.</li>
</ol>
<p>These two conditions nearly pin down <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi mathvariant="script">L</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathcal L</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathcal">L</span></span></span></span>, and the simplest function meeting them is the Born–Infeld Lagrangian — the next note, <a href="/rome-wiki/articles/string-theory/part-iv-branes-and-duality/21-born-infeld-and-brane-dynamics/born-infeld-action/" class="wiki-link">Born-Infeld action</a>.</p>
<h2 id="open-questions">Open questions</h2>
<ul>
<li>General nonlinear theories exhibit <strong>birefringence</strong>: different polarizations travel at different speeds through a background field. Born–Infeld is the special member with <em>no</em> birefringence — a distinguishing virtue not derived here (it comes from the specific <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>p</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">p^2</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1.0085em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8141em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight">2</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> term in the BI Lagrangian).</li>
<li>In <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>D</mi><mo mathvariant="normal">≠</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">D\ne 4</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8889em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0278em;">D</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel"><span class="mrel"><span class="mord vbox"><span class="thinbox"><span class="rlap"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8889em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="inner"><span class="mord"><span class="mrel"></span></span></span><span class="fix"></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mspace nobreak"></span><span class="mrel">=</span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">4</span></span></span></span> the counting of invariants changes — there is no single dual scalar <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">p</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.625em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span></span></span></span> — yet the determinant form of Born–Infeld still makes sense in any dimension. The clean "function of <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>s</mi><mo separator="true">,</mo><mi>p</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">s,p</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.625em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">s</span><span class="mpunct">,</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span></span></span></span>" statement is special to four dimensions.</li>
</ul>
<h2 id="exercise">Exercise</h2>
<p>Show that the Maxwell Lagrangian <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi \tfrac12(E^2-B^2)</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8333em;vertical-align:-0.15em;"></span><span Concretely, use <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mover accent="true"><mi>D</mi><mo>⃗</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><mi mathvariant="script">L</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">/</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><mover accent="true"><mi>E</mi><mo>⃗</mo></mover></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\vec D=\partial\mathcal L/\partial\vec E</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.9663em;"></span><span class="mord accent"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.9663em;"><span style="top:-3em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0278em;">D</span></span><span style="top:-3.2523em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="accent-body" style="left:-0.1799em;"><span class="overlay" style="height:0.714em;width:0.471em;"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="0.471em" height="0.714em" style="width:0.471em" viewBox="0 0 471 714" preserveAspectRatio="xMinYMin"><path d="M377 20c0-5.333 1.833-10 5.5-14S391 0 397 0c4.667 0 8.667 1.667 12 5

<details class="callout callout-success">
<summary class="callout-title">Solution</summary>
<p>Differentiate componentwise. Since <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="script">L</mi><mi>M</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mstyle stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathcal L_M=\tfrac12(E_x^2+E_y^2+E_z^2) - \tfrac12(B_x^2+B_y^2+B_z^2)</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8333em;vertical-align:-0.15em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathcal">L</span><span mtight"><span class="mord mtight">2</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span>); its sign is fixed by Lorentz invariance, and it is exactly the sign that makes the energy positive. The apparent sign flip between <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi mathvariant="script">L</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathcal L</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathcal">L</span></span></span></span> and <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi mathvariant="script">H</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathcal H</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathcal" style="margin-right:0.0097em;">H</span></span></span></span> on the <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>B</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">B</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0502em;">B</span></span></span></span> terms is the Legendre transform doing its job.</p>
